ASEAN経済は国ごとにばらつき。ベトナムは輸出や
鉱工業生産の好調を背景に高成⾧持続。フィリピン
では食料品やエネルギー価格高騰が消費の重石に。
タイでも原油高を背景にインフレ圧力が強まる
APAC
賃金改善が続く一方、個人消費の回復に弱さ。実
質消費支出は前年比減少が継続。消費者マインド

も小幅悪化し、物価高への警戒が根強い。景気動
向指数は低下したものの、基調判断は改善を維持
日
本
先週（10/5～10/12）発表の主な経済指標
米国では金融政策運営の変化と財政懸念、欧州では財政不
安が金利上昇圧力に。欧州では⾧期金利上昇による景気下
押し圧力が、ECBの追加利上げの必要性を低下させる可能性
金利・コスト上昇などの逆風下でも、設備投資意欲は旺盛。家
計の節約志向や企業間の格差は残るものの、価格転嫁・賃上
げも継続しており、日銀の想定するシナリオに沿った動き

米国：中間選挙を目前に住宅市場は軟調、アフォーダビリティへの不満が共和党への逆風に
 住宅ローン金利は足元で再び上昇
― 借入コストの負担増により、足元の住宅ローン申請件数は減少
 物価上昇が生活コストを押し上げるなか、金利上昇による住宅取得環境の悪化も家計の不満を助長する要因に
― トランプ大統領の生活費への政策対応は約7割が「期待より悪い」と評価しており、支持率は低下基調
―11月に控える中間選挙では、アフォーダビリティへの対応が引き続き主要な論点

米国：金融政策・財政の両面から米長期金利に上昇圧力
 住宅ローン金利の基準となる米国10年債利回りは9月以降水準を切り上げ、足元（10/6時点）では5.3％近傍に
― 構成要素をみると、追加利上げ観測の高まりを受け、実質予想短期金利が上昇。ウォーシュFRB議長の就任以降、フォ
ワードガイダンスの後退を背景に、経済指標への金利の感応度が高まっている可能性
― 加えて、足元ではタームプレミアムも拡大。中間選挙を控えた家計支援策への思惑などから、財政懸念が意識されてい

る模様。トランプ大統領は10/2、Medicare加入者2,000万人超への90ドルの一時金支給を発表
5

欧州：長期金利上昇を受け、一部ECB高官は追加利上げの必要性低下を指摘
6
 フランスでは国内政治・財政面の不安から長期金利が上昇。イタリア・ギリシャなど周辺の高債務国にも波及
― フランス国債の対独利回り差は急拡大し、一時欧州債務危機（2012年）以来の水準に。その後、大統領選の有力候補と
目されるルペン氏（国民連合）が財政赤字を縮小する意欲を示し、拡大はひとまず一服
―ECBによる介入（国債買い入れ等）の可能性については、複数の高官が検討段階にないとの見方を示す
 長期金利の上昇は需要を抑制。ECB高官からは追加利上げの必要性が低下するとの見方も
― エネルギー高によるインフレ圧力と金利上昇による景気下押し圧力が併存。ECBの政策判断は一段と難しくなる可能性
仏長期金利上昇の経済影響（左）と直近のECB高官発言（右）
（出所）LSEGより、みずほ総合研究所調査部作成

### Page 8

7
日本：金利・コスト上昇下でも企業活動は底堅く、設備投資意欲の強さを再確認
 10月のさくらレポートではAI関連需要の拡大とその裾野の広がりを背景に、企業活動の底堅さを確認
― 同一の企業コメントに登場するテーマの組み合わせを集計した共起分析では、「金利×設備投資」の組み合わせが増加。
金利上昇への意識が強まる一方、成長・省人化投資の旺盛なニーズは継続
 賃上げや資産効果が消費を下支えする一方、物価高を背景とした消費の二極化が継続
― 企業は価格転嫁を積極化するも、消費者の節約志向が根強く、商品競争力や顧客層によって転嫁余力に格差
⇒景気・物価は強弱入り混じるも、日銀の想定するシナリオに概ね沿った展開。12月利上げ見通しは不変
企業コメントの共起関係の変化
（注） 同一の箇条書きコメントに、双方のテーマのキーワードがある場合を1件として計上。
なお、テーマ間の因果関係を意味するものではない
（出所） 日本銀行「地域経済報告」より、みずほ総合研究所調査部作成

0 10 20 30

AI関連 × 設備投資
中東情勢 × 価格転嫁
節約志向 × 消費・販売
7月
10月
（件）
設備投資における金利負
担、企業収益における価
格転嫁の重要性を示唆
 AI関連需要は想定を上回るペースで拡大。半導
体製造装置に加え、工作機械や光ファイバー関連
製品など幅広い分野に波及
 金利・建設コストの上昇下でも、競争力維持に必
要な投資を継続。一方、採算悪化や調達コスト増
から投資を縮小・延期する動きも
設備投資・生産
 原材料・人件費の上昇を受け、企業の価格改定は
機動化。値上げ頻度を引き上げる企業がみられる
一方、消費者の価格感応度も高まる
 価格転嫁が進み賃上げ原資を確保できる企業が
ある一方、転嫁不足から利益を削って賃上げを続
ける企業も
価格転嫁・賃金
 株高による資産効果や賃上げを背景に、高額品・
高機能家電・旅行などの需要は堅調
 一方、物価高の長期化を受け、高年層を中心に生
活防衛意識が強まる。低価格品へのシフトや値上

世界経済：エネルギー・食料の供給ショックによる負担は、所得階層や国によって格差
 生活費高騰に伴うインフレの影響は所得階層により格差
― 食料・エネルギー支出の割合が高い低所得層は負担が大。IMFは低所得層に絞った所得移転が望ましい対応と指摘
 エネルギー・食料の供給ショックによる影響は国家間でも格差
― 先進国では、エネルギー供給ショックによる消費の下押し効果は低所得層で特に大きい
― 食料品への支出割合が高い新興国・低所得途上国では、食料供給ショックによる消費の下押し効果が相対的に大きい

 タイでは過去にも大規模洪水が発生。2011年には、日系自動車企業の部材調達が滞り、日本国内生産に影響した経験も
―2011年の洪水発生時には、工場の被災や物流停滞、人員不足が発生。タイの製造業稼働率は一時30％近く低下
― 当時浸水した工業団地では日系企業約450社が被災。タイ製部品の調達難により、日本でも完成車が一時減産に

―10月頭時点では各社、大規模な設備被害や減産は見込まず、日本の部材調達への影響も限定的となる見込み
― ただし、10月中旬にかけ更なる大雨が予想されており、操業停止の長期化や被害地域の拡大には依然警戒が必要

日本：個人消費は耐久財特需が剥落。今後は良好な所得環境を背景に緩やかに拡大
12
 8月の消費活動指数は、耐久財特需の剥落などにより、前年比＋0.9％と伸びが鈍化
― 耐久財消費は春先の自動車特需の反動から減少。食品などの非耐久財も、根強い節約志向を背景に弱含みが継続
― 底堅く推移してきたサービス消費も、台風・大雨や猛暑による外出控えの影響などから拡大ペースが鈍化
 今後は良好な所得環境が消費を下支え。個人消費は緩慢なペースながら、底堅く推移する見通し
― 実質所定内給与はプラス圏の伸びを維持しており、基調的な所得環境は良好。増加基調の購買力が消費を下支え
― ただし、インフレ率の再上昇で消費の回復ペースは緩慢。マインドの改善が一服したことも回復ペースを抑える要因に

よる海外消費（アウトバウンド消費）を加えるための調整要因。アウトバウンド消費は
SNAの家計最終消費支出に含まれるが、GDPの計算上は輸入として控除される

日本：景気ウォッチャーは消費の緩やかな持ち直しを示唆も、家計・企業とも先行きには慎重
13
 家計動向（現状判断DI）は改善。9月の消費回復を示唆するも、回復ペースは緩慢
― 特にサービス関連の上昇が顕著。8月に増勢が一服したサービス消費は、大型連休や秋の行楽需要を受け持ち直し
― ただし、先行き判断DIは低下しており、個人消費全体でみれば緩やかな回復であることを示唆
 一方、企業動向は、現状・先行きとも慎重。景気回復の勢いは強くない
― 企業動向（現状判断DI）は製造業・非製造業ともに低下。AI関連など需要は堅調も、資材高などの供給制約が重石に

― 先行き判断についても、原材料高や金利上昇、中東情勢の不透明感などが重石となっている模様

か？

## Document 034: 2026年10月5日國際經濟金融週報

Source PDF: all report pdf/2026年10月5日國際經濟金融週報.pdf
Report source: Mega Bank (國際經濟金融週報)
Date: 2026-10-05
Pages: 10
Extraction status: ok

### Page 1

本報告係根據研究資料及各項可靠數據作成，兆豐銀行保留報告內容所有權利，未經本行許可，請勿引
用相關內容。

兆豐國際商業銀行 徵信處 
中華民國一一五年十月五日

國際經濟金融週報

### Page 2

本報告係根據研究資料及各項可靠數據作成，兆豐銀行保留報告內容所有權利，未經本行許可，請勿引
用相關內容。

1

國際經濟金融週報內容摘要 
美 
國 
美國經濟維持溫和擴張，勞動市場降溫與通膨黏著牽動聯準會政策 
經濟 
⚫ 2026 年第 2 季美國實質 GDP 季增年率終值上修至 2.2%， 惟低於第1 季
之 2.5%。成長主要由個人消費及固定投資帶動，其中消費貢獻 2.51 個
百分點；惟進口大增 12.6%，致淨出口拖累 1.10 個百分點。另實質私人
國內最終銷售成長 4.6%，顯示內需仍具韌性。 
⚫ 9 月美國非農就業僅增 2.9 萬人，遠低於前月及近一年平均，失業率升
至 4.2%， 薪資增幅續降 ， 顯示企業聘僱動能轉弱 。 惟勞動市場尚未明顯
惡化，後續仍須關注就業降溫對聯準會利率決策之影響。 
⚫ 8 月美國職位空缺數由 733.5 萬個降至 707.9 萬個，職缺率降至 4.3%，
顯示企業徵才需求略為降溫；惟聘僱人數增至 519.2 萬人。主動離職及
裁員規模同步下降，反映勞工轉職意願與企業人力調整趨於審慎，勞動
市場供需大致維持平衡。 
⚫ 9 月美國 ISM 製造業採購經理人指數為 54.5，連續第九個月維持擴張。
新訂單、就業及未完成訂單同步改善，顯示需求仍具韌性；惟生產、出
口及進口動能略為放緩，且原物料成本持續攀升，加上關稅政策、地緣
政治及供應鏈不確定性仍存，製造業仍面臨部分壓力。 
⚫ 8 月美國 PCE 物價指數月增 0.3%、年增 3.4%，核心 PCE 月增 0.2%、
年增 3.0%， 顯示通膨大致持穩但短期物價壓力略升 。 另一方面 ， 個人消
費支出月增 0.9%、實質消費成長 0.6%，帶動儲蓄率降至 4.1%，顯示家
庭需求仍具韌性；後續須關注消費熱度及通膨對聯準會政策之影響。 
重要 
事件 
⚫ 近期聯準會官員立場趨於分歧，理事 Michael Barr 認為能源價格及 AI
投資熱潮可能延緩通膨降溫 ， 未來仍可能進一步升息 ； 惟John Williams、
Philip Jefferson 及 Michelle Bowman 等官員則主張暫無急於升息的必要 ，
傾向觀察後續經濟數據。加以 9 月非農就業明顯放緩，市場對 10 月升
息預期大幅降溫，焦點轉向 12 月會議。 
⚫ 台積電傳評估與馬斯克旗下 Terafab 合作赴德州設廠，雙方仍處討論階
段。合作可能由台積電主導營運，或由 Terafab 持多數股權、台積電提
供技術 。 若成真 ， 將擴大台積電美國布局 ， 並支援Tesla、SpaceX 及 xAI
晶片需求。

一、 美國—美國經濟維持溫和擴張，勞動市場降溫與通膨黏著牽動聯準會政策 
2026 年第二季美國實質 GDP 季增年率終值由前次估計之 1.5%上修至 2.2%，
惟仍低於第一季之 2.5%， 顯示經濟持續擴張但成長動能略有放緩 。 此次上修主要
反映投資、消費及政府支出優於原先估計。就成長結構觀察，個人消費支出仍為
主要動能，貢獻 2.51 個百分點，其中服務及商品消費分別貢獻 1.57 及 0.94 個百
分點；私人投資貢獻 0.82 個百分點，以設備投資及智慧財產權投資表現強勁，惟
庫存調整拖累 0.53 個百分點。對外貿易方面，出口成長 5.0%，但進口大幅增加
12.6%， 致淨出口拖累GDP 成長 1.10 個百分點 。 另反映內需基本面的實質私人國
內最終銷售成長 4.6%， 高於第一季之1.8%， 顯示民間需求明顯增強 。 物價方面 ，
第 2 季 PCE 及核心 PCE 物價指數分別上升 5.0%及 3.3%， 顯示通膨壓力仍偏高 。
整體而言，美國經濟仍由消費與固定投資支撐，惟進口快速增加及通膨黏著性，
仍為後續經濟前景的重要觀察重點。 
2026 年 9 月美國非農就業人數增加 2.9 萬人 ， 明顯低於上月修正後的13.3 萬
人，也低於過去 12 個月平均每月增加 4.5 萬人的水準；失業率小幅升至 4.2%，
整體仍維持在 3 月以來 4.1%至 4.3%的區間，反映勞動市場整體變化有限，惟新
增就業力道已轉趨疲弱。家庭調查方面，勞動參與率升至 61.8%，就業人口比為
59.2%，均較上月小幅上升；長期失業人數約 194.4 萬人，占總失業人口 27.1%，
大致維持穩定。產業別方面，主要產業就業變化有限，其中醫療保健增加 1.7 萬
人、營建業增加 1.1 萬人、製造業增加 0.9 萬人，金融活動則減少 0.7 萬人，政府
部門減少 1.7 萬人。薪資方面，私人部門平均時薪升至 37.81 美元，月增 0.1%、
年增 3.0%，平均每週工時維持 34.4 小時。另 7 月與 8 月新增就業合計下修 6 萬
人，反映前二月就業動能較初估疲弱。整體而言，9 月美國新增就業明顯放緩、
失業率小幅上升且薪資增速降溫，企業聘僱動能較先前轉弱；惟失業率仍維持於
近期區間，勞動市場尚未出現明顯惡化跡象，後續仍須關注企業聘僱需求是否持
續降溫，以及對聯準會利率決策的影響。 
2026 年 8 月美國職位空缺數(Job Openings) 由上月修正後之 733.5 萬個減少
25.6 萬個至 707.9 萬個 ， 職缺率由4.4%降至 4.3%， 顯示企業用人需求略有降溫 ；

惟聘僱人數由 514.6 萬人小幅增加至 519.2 萬人，聘僱率由 3.2%升至 3.3%。總離
職人數由 512.8 萬人減少至 507.0 萬人，離職率維持於 3.2%。就離職結構觀察，
主動離職(Quits)人數由 308.9 萬人降至 306.6 萬人，主動離職率維持 1.9%；裁員
與解僱(Layoffs and Discharges) 人數則由 170.2 萬人降至 164.1 萬人，裁員率由
1.1%降至 1.0%。產業別方面，職位空缺增加主要來自零售業、金融保險業、資訊
業及休閒餐飲業 ； 聘僱人數則以政府部門及製造業增幅較為明顯 。 整體而言 ，8 月
美國職缺數較前月下滑，反映企業徵才需求略有降溫，但聘僱活動仍維持穩定，
且主動離職與裁員規模同步下降，顯示勞工轉職意願及企業人力調整趨於審慎，
勞動市場供需仍大致維持平衡。 
2026 年 9 月美國 ISM 製造業採購經理人指數(Manufacturing PMI) 為 54.5，
較上月之 54.6 微降 0.1 點，惟已連續第九個月維持於 50 以上擴張區間，顯示製
造業景氣持續擴張。細項觀察，新訂單指數由53.7 升至 55.3，需求動能續增；生
產指數由 58.3 降至 56.7，雖仍維持擴張，但成長速度略有放緩；就業指數由51.2
升至 52.7，反映企業聘僱需求持續增加。供應商交貨指數由 59.3 微降至 59.0，交
貨延遲情況仍存；庫存指數則由 50.6 降至 48.6，企業備貨態度轉趨保守。此外，
價格指數由 71.1 升至 77.9，原物料及投入成本壓力升高；未完成訂單指數由51.8
升至 56.4，訂單去化變慢。另一方面，新出口訂單指數由53.2 降至 50.9，進口指
數亦由 52.5 降至 51.0，外需成長動能及企業備料需求略為放緩。整體而言，9 月
美國製造業活動持續穩健擴張，新訂單、就業及未完成訂單同步改善，顯示製造
業景氣仍具韌性；惟出口及進口增速放緩、投入成本壓力升高，加上關稅政策、
地緣政治風險及供應鏈不確定性仍存，後續發展仍值得持續關注。 
2026 年 8 月美國個人消費支出(PCE)物價指數月增率由上月之 0.1%升至 0.3%，
年增率維持於 3.4%； 扣除食品及能源後之核心PCE 月增率由 0.1%升至 0.2%，年
增率亦持平於 3.0%，顯示整體通膨大致持穩，惟短期物價漲勢較前月略有回升。
另一方面，個人所得月增 0.2%，可支配所得(DPI)月增 0.3%，實質可支配所得持
平；個人消費支出月增 0.9%，實質個人消費支出成長 0.6%，顯示民間消費表現
明顯回溫。由於消費增幅高於所得增幅，個人儲蓄率由前月之 4.6%降至 4.1%。
整體而言，8 月美國通膨表現大致持穩，但消費支出顯著回升，反映家庭需求仍
具韌性；後續仍須持續關注消費熱度、通膨走勢及其對聯準會貨幣政策路徑之影
響。

近期聯準會官員談話呈現鷹鴿分歧。聯準會理事 Michael Barr 認為，中東局
勢推升能源價格，以及 AI 相關投資熱潮帶動需求增長，均可能阻礙通膨回落至
2%目標 ， 未來仍不排除進一步升息的必要性 ； 惟 紐約聯準銀行總裁 John Williams、
聯準會副主席 Philip Jefferson 及金融監理副主席 Michelle Bowman 則強調，在 9
月升息後，當前並無急迫性立即進一步收緊政策，應先觀察後續通膨、就業及金
融市場數據變化，再評估是否有進一步調整利率的必要。受官員談話轉趨審慎，
以及 9 月非農就業表現疲弱影響，市場對 10 月 FOMC 會議再度升息的預期明顯
降溫，政策焦點逐漸轉向 12 月會議及年底前是否仍有一次升息空間。 
晶圓代工龍頭台積電傳出正評估與馬斯克旗下 Terafab 合作，在美國德州布
局晶圓廠，馬斯克也於社群平台 X 證實雙方正在討論，並表示 「目前僅在討論階
段 ， 但可能會有進展」 。 目前浮上檯面的合作模式包括由台積電持有並營運新廠 ，
SpaceX 或 Terafab 透過入股及承諾採購晶圓參與；另一方案則由 Terafab 掌握多
數股權 ， 台積電提供製程技術與晶圓廠營運經驗 。Terafab 旨在因應 Tesla、SpaceX
及 xAI 快速成長的 AI 晶片需求，首期投資規模達 168 億美元。若合作成真，不
僅可望成為台積電繼亞利桑那州後在美國的新布局，也凸顯先進製程與穩定晶片
供應的重要性，但目前具體合作方式、時程與投資內容仍未正式定案。 
二、歐元區—法國殖利率大幅上升，不利於投資及消費表現 
 歐元區 9 月 HICP 通膨 、 核心通膨(不含食品及能源)分別上升 0.6、0.1 個百分
點至 3.8%、2.5%， 主因國際原油價格上漲 ， 使能源通膨上升4.5 個百分點至 18.8%，
並透過肥料、物流成本上漲，間接推升食品通膨 0.3 個百分點至 1.4%。另外，服
務通膨上升 0.2 個百分點至 3.2%，近三個月服務通膨大致呈現橫向波動，部分反
映旅遊旺季因素影響。基於布蘭特原油持續位於每桶 90 美元以上，預期年底前
仍將對通膨帶來額外壓力，後續須關注能源漲價可能會透過運輸、物流等成本管
道傳導至核心商品及服務價格，進而提高核心通膨黏性。 
 其他經濟數據包含 ：(1)歐元區 8 月失業率持平 6.4%， 失業人數增加2.6 萬人 ，
主要是義大利勞動參與人數增加所致，不過 ，基於PMI 顯示近期德國勞動雇用需
求持續增溫，將有助於穩定勞動市場表現；(2)歐元區第二季家庭儲蓄率下降 0.2
個百分點至 14.2%，顯示通膨壓力削弱民眾購買力，致儲蓄暫時性減少；(3)歐元
區第二季房價年增率為 4%， 較第一季降0.6 個百分點 ， 主因法國中古屋面臨嚴格

環保法規，須負擔高昂翻修成本，使法國房價呈現連三季下滑所拖累。 
 近一週法國 10 年期公債殖利率升至 4.9%以上，與德國公債殖利率差距擴大
至 125 個基點以上，主要反映市場對法國主權公債可持續性疑慮升溫。儘管目前
政府計畫將 2027 年財政赤字縮限至 5%，惟政府在國會並無穩定多數席次，即使
能以行政命令強行通過預算 ， 也可能面臨倒閣風險 ；此外 ，市場對於2027 年 4 月
總統大選後，新總統能否取得國會足夠支持，推動削減赤字與控制債務的改革亦
缺乏信心。因此未來法國仍可能面臨殖利率進一步上升與財政壓力加劇的風險，
暫時不利於投資及消費信心。 
三、日本—景氣溫和復甦，但消費放緩與通膨升溫風險升高 
 8 月工業生產月減 1.7%，主要係受到該月地震及豪雨等天然災害影響，導致
部分工廠暫時停產。從細項來看，汽車業受停工影響最為明顯，產量下滑6.8%，
電子產品產量亦減少 1.9%。 惟生產機械產量連續三個月成長 ， 加上核心資本財出
貨持續增加，顯示企業投資需求仍相當強勁，有助於支撐後續工業生產回升。從
企業預期來看，製造商預期9 月產量將成長 3.2%，顯示生產活動可望迅速回溫，
在供應鏈逐步恢復、庫存回補需求持續，以及穩健的企業資本支出支撐下，預計
第三季工業生產仍可維持正成長，且成長動能可望延續至年底。 
 8 月零售月減 1.2%，雖然表現略優於市場預期，但仍顯示民間消費動能正在
放緩。此次疲弱主要原因來自耐久財銷售下滑，其中家電銷售因空調等產品在換
機需求告一段落後明顯回落，汽車銷售也隨政府刺激措施效應逐漸消退而連續第
四個月下降。相較之下，食品、服裝等非耐久財銷售雖同步走弱，但降幅相對有
限 ， 反映薪資成長仍對消費提供部分支撐 。惟不利天候與通膨壓力仍是主要風險 ，
8 月至 9 月持續豪雨已影響服裝等非必需消費，而除米價以外的食品及商品價格
持續上漲，也正侵蝕家庭實質購買力，恐限制消費者信心與消費復甦力道。 
 9 月東京核心通膨年增率升至 2.7%，雖然東京都免費托兒及免費水費政策造
成的基期效應貢獻約 0.5 個百分點，但通膨升溫主因仍是價格上漲已由能源與食
品擴散至整體經濟。受石油價格上揚帶動，企業開始加速將原物料及薪資成本轉
嫁給消費者，帶動商品與服務價格普遍走高；租金通膨更升至 2%，創 1994 年以
來新高，公共交通及醫療等項目也開始調漲。隨著東京的物價壓力逐步反映至全
國數據，日本央行進一步升息的可能性升高。

四、中國—為刺激內需、引導產業走向，人行漸近式放寬政策工具 
9 月官方製造業 PMI 上升 0.3 點至 50.1，為近三個月高點，新訂單、出口訂
單指數分別為 50.5、50，並帶動生產指數上升至 51.7，不過，庫存指數下降、就
業指數加速衰退、企業展望減弱，皆拖累 PMI 指數水準。此外，投入、產出物價
指數分別上升至 60.8、54，顯示成本壓力加劇，但售價調整速度依然較為遲緩，
不利於獲利表現。官方非製造業 PMI 上升 1.2 點至 50.2，其中建築業活動指數大
增 3.4 點至 50.3，脫離連續八個月的衰退區間，主要是政府加大財政刺激力度，
推動重大建設開工；非製造業的從業人員指數為 45.9，顯示就業情況仍顯嚴峻，
亦較不利於內需表現。 
鑒於內需動能疲弱，人行於 9 月 29 日宣布下調抵押補充貸款(該貸款本質是
將資金引導至政府希望發展的領域)利率自 1.75%降至 1.50%，並擴大適用範圍，
將水網、新型電網、算力網、新一代通信網、城市地下管網及物流網等建設納入
支持，有助於提振內需動能。未來若內需復甦力道不如預期，不排除人行將進一
步降準或擴大結構性貨幣工具支持經濟；至於全面降息則仍須兼顧人民幣匯率及
中美利差等因素，可能性相對較低。 
五、印度及東南亞—泰國、菲律賓出口均達 2 成以上的高速成長 
(1)新加坡：受服務業、零售暨其他商品業，以及食品價格漲幅擴大影響，8 月消
費者物價指數(CPI)年增率升至 2.3%、不包括住宿及個人交通費用之核心 CPI 年
增率升至 2.2%， 連續3 個月上升並創 2 年新高 。 受全球能源價格居高不下及氣候
條件不佳影響，預期新加坡進口食品、商品及服務成本將持續上升，惟國內生產
率成長及政府補貼政策將抑制部分服務業通膨，新加坡金管局(MAS)或在 10 月
「略微」緊縮貨幣政策。(2)泰國：8 月出口額年增 24.3%(346 億美元)，主要由工
業產品出口成長 27.1%所帶動，其中電腦及相關設備（年增 42.1%）、電話及零
件（151.2%） 及電路板 （19.2%） 增幅最為顯著，對美國出口年增48.7%、對中國
和日本分別成長 15.5%、19.6%。8 月進口額年增 25.1%(371 億美元)，以燃料 （年
增 42.8%） 、 資本貨物 （33.9%） 、 其他貨物 （27.4%） 、 原材料和中間產品 （21.3%）、
運輸設備（13.6%）為主。進出口相抵，8 月貿易逆差 24.8 億美元，較 7 月略收
窄，為連續第 11 個月出現逆差。(3)菲律賓：8 月出口額大增 27.8%(91 億美元)，
創兩年新高，主要得益於電子產品（年增 59.9%） ，尤其是半導體（73.5%）及黃

2026 年 9 月日銀短観 
AI 需要で製造業は改善も、先行きはコスト増や金利上昇で業況悪化

経済調査部 エコノミスト 中村 華奈子

[要約] 
 2026 年 9 月日銀短観では、大企業製造業の業況判断DI（最近）は＋24％pt（前回差＋
2％pt）に改善した一方、大企業非製造業は＋35％pt（同▲2％pt）へと悪化した。製
造業では、旺盛な AI 関連需要が景況感を押し上げた。非製造業では、燃料費や人件費
などのコスト増が重石となり、 「宿泊・飲食サービス」や「小売」など個人消費に関連
する業種を中心に業況が悪化した。 
 業況判断 DI（先行き）は、大企業製造業で＋21％pt（今回差▲3％pt） 、大企業非製造
業で＋30％pt（同▲5％pt）といずれも悪化が見込まれている。中東情勢に収束の兆し
が見られないなか、原油価格の高止まりに伴うコスト増 への懸念や、金利上昇による
資金調達コスト増大や需要の下押しへの警戒感から、幅広い業種で慎重な見方が広が
った。 
 2026 年度の設備投資計画（全規模全産業、含む土地、ソフトウェアと研究開発投資額
は含まない）は前年度比＋7.6％へと上方修正されたが、おおむね例年通りの修正幅だ
った。省力化投資や更新投資、能力増強 投資等への意欲は引き続き強いとみられ、大
企業では製造業・非製造業ともに前年度比 2 桁増の計画が維持されたほか、中小企業
でも、非製造業を中心に前回調査から上方修正された。 
 企業の販売価格の見通しと物価全般の見通しはいずれも高水準にあり、企業の価格設
定行動は今後も積極的な状態が続くとみられる。資金繰り判断 DI（最近）や金融機関
の貸出態度判断 DI（最近）は横ばい圏で推移しており、金融環境はおおむね緩和的な
状態が維持されているといえる。もっとも、借入金利水準判断 DI（最近）は大企業・
中小企業ともに上昇し、先行きも一段の上昇が見込まれるなど、利上げの影響が企業
にも広がりつつある。

【業況判断 DI（最近） 】AI関連需要が追い風となる一方、コスト増で非製造業は悪化 
2026 年 9 月の日銀短観では、大企業製造業の業況判断DI（最近）は＋24％pt（前回差＋2％
pt） 、大企業非製造業では＋35％pt（同▲2％pt）となった。製造業では、旺盛なAI関連需要が
景況感を押し上げた。非製造業では、燃料費や人件費などのコスト増加が収益の重石となり、
個人消費に関連する業種を中心に業況が悪化した。 
大企業製造業のうち、 「素材業種」の業況判断DI（最近）は前回差＋7％ptだった。原材料コ
スト上昇分の価格転嫁が進展し、「石油・石炭製品」 （同＋27％pt）や「鉄鋼」 （同＋12％pt）
などの業況判断 DI（最近）が上昇したほか、 「窯業・土石製品」 （同＋11％pt）や「非鉄金属」
（同＋9％pt） 、 「化学」 （同＋6％pt）といったAI関連の素材を扱う業種でも業況が改善した。 
「加工業種」の業況判断 DI（最近）は前回差＋2％pt だった。 「生産用機械」 （同＋7％pt）や
「電気機械」 （同＋4％pt）では、半導体関連を中心とする AI 関連需要を追い風に業況が改善し
た。他方、 「食料品」 （同▲6％pt）では原材料・エネルギーコストの上昇が重石となったとみ
られるほか、 「造船・重機等」 （同▲3％pt）や「自動車」 （同▲1％pt）でも業況判断DI（最近）
が低下した。 
大企業非製造業では、「宿泊・飲食サービス」 （前回差▲8％pt）や「小売」 （同▲4％pt）、
「対個人サービス」 （同▲3％pt）など、個人消費に関連する業種で業況判断 DI（最近）が低下
した。燃料費や人件費の上昇に加え、訪日客数の停滞などが景況感を下押ししたとみられる。
また、 「運輸・郵便」 （同▲4％pt）では燃料費の上昇、 「物品賃貸」 （同▲4％pt）では金利上昇
に伴う調達
コストの増加が業況の悪化につながった可能性がある。

【業況判断 DI（先行き）】コスト高や金利上昇への警戒感から幅広い業種で悪化見込み 
大企業製造業の業況判断 DI（先行き）は＋21％pt（今回差▲3％pt） 、大企業非製造業は＋
30％pt（同▲5％pt）と、いずれも悪化が見込まれている。 
大企業製造業では、 「素材業種」が＋15％pt（今回差▲10％pt） 、 「加工業種」が＋23％pt
（同▲2％pt）と素材業種の低下幅が大きい。中東情勢に収束の兆しが見られないなか、原油価
格の高止まりに伴うコスト増や原材料の供給制約に対する警戒感が重石となったとみられる。
一方、加工業種では好調なAI関連需要もあってDIの低下は小幅だった。 
大企業非製造業では、 「物品賃貸」 （今回差▲19％pt） 、 「不動産」 （同▲11％pt）、「 建 設 」（ 同
▲8％pt）など幅広い業種で業況判断 DI（先行き）が低下した。物品賃貸や不動産では、金利
上昇による資金調達コストの上昇や、それに伴う設備・住宅需要の下押しが懸念されたとみら
れる。物価上昇に伴う家計の実質購買力の低下や最低賃金引き上げに伴う人件費負担の増加に
対する懸念も、幅広い業種で慎重な見方が広がった一因と考えられる。

【売上高・経常利益計画】全規模全産業は増収増益も、円安の影響には注意が必要 
2026 年度における全規模全産業の売上高は前年度比＋4.1％の増収、経常利益は同＋2.2％の
増益が見込まれている。2026年度の想定為替レート（円/ドル、全規模・全産業）は154.23 円
であり、足元の水準（158 円/ドル台）よりも円高水準にある。今後、企業の想定為替レートが
円安方向へ修正されると、輸出比率の高い製造業を中心に、売上高や経常利益計画が上方修正
される可能性がある。他方、輸入燃料や原材料への依存度が高い運輸・郵便や食料品などの業
種では、円安に伴うコスト増加を受けて経常利益計画が下方修正されるリスクにも注意が必要
だ。

【需給・価格判断】中小企業では先行きの価格転嫁の進展が見込まれる 
大企業の「国内での製商品・サービス需給判断 DI（最近） 」は、製造業で▲1％pt（前回差＋
3％pt） 、非製造業で＋4％pt（同▲2％pt）だった。先行きは、製造業で▲1％pt（今回差±0％
pt） 、非製造業で＋3％pt（同▲1％pt）と見込まれている。 
大企業の「海外での製商品需給判断 DI（最近）」は、 「素材業種」で▲8％pt（前回差±0％
pt） 、 「加工業種」で＋3％pt（同＋3％pt）であった。 「海外での製商品需給判断DI（先行き）」
は、 「素材業種」と「加工業種」のいずれも今回差＋1％pt と改善した。 
大企業の「仕入価格判断 DI（最近） 」は、製造業で＋59％pt（前回差▲3％pt） 、非製造業で
＋54％pt（同▲2％pt）であった。先行きでは、製造業が＋55％pt（今回差▲4％pt） 、非製造
業は＋54％pt（同±0％pt）となった。大企業の「販売価格判断 DI（最近） 」は、製造業が＋
40％pt（前回差±0％pt） 、非製造業が＋38％pt（同▲2％pt）で あった。先行きは、製造業が
＋40％pt（今回差±0％pt） 、非製造業が＋40％pt（同＋2％pt）となった。 
企業の
交易条件（販売価格判断 DI と仕入価格判断 DI の差）を確認すると、中小企業では製
造業、非製造業ともに先行きの仕入価格判断DI の変化幅を販売価格判断 DI の上昇幅が上回っ
ており、価格転嫁の進展が見込まれる。 
企業の物価見通し（全規模全産業）を見ると、販売価格の見通しと物価全般の見通しはいず
れも高水準にある。企業はこれまでのコスト増を持続的な物価上昇圧力として認識している可
能性があり、今後も企業の価格設定行動を後押しするとみられる。

された。

【雇用判断】雇用の不足感は中小企業を中心に一段と強まる見込み 
雇用の過不足感を示す「雇用人員判断 DI」 （最近）は、大企業製造業で▲22％pt（前回差▲
1％pt）、大企業非製造業で▲37％pt（同▲1％pt）だった。中小企業では、製造業で▲32 ％pt
（同▲4％pt） 、非製造業で▲47％pt（同▲1％pt）となった。 
先行きは、大企業製造業で▲ 27％pt（今回差▲5％pt）、大企業非製造業で▲ 37％pt（同±
0％pt）、中小企業製造業で▲37％pt（同▲5％pt）、中小企業非製造業で▲50％pt（同▲3％pt）
と見込まれている。中小企業を中心に雇用の不足感は一段と強まる見込みだ。

図表11：雇用人員判断DI

【企業金融】緩和的な金融環境はおおむね維持も、借入金利が上昇 
資金繰り判断 DI（最近）は、大企業全産業で＋13％pt（前回差＋1％pt） 、中小企業全産業で
＋8％pt（同±0％pt）となった。また、金融機関の貸出態度判断 DI（最近）は、大企業全産業
で＋12％pt（前回差▲1％pt） 、中小企業全産業で＋12％pt（同±0％pt）と、横ばい圏で推移
した。金融環境はおおむね緩和的な状態が維持されているといえる。 
他方、借入金利水準判断 DI（最近）は、大企業全産業で＋65％pt（前回差＋6％pt） 、中小企
業全産業で＋70％pt（同＋9％pt）と上昇した。先行きについても、大企業全産業 で＋67％pt
（今回差＋2％pt） 、中小企業全産業で＋74％pt（同＋4％pt）と一段の上昇が見込まれている。
資金繰りへの影響は現時点では限定的であるものの、利上げの進展を受けて、中小企業を中心
に資金調達コストの上昇圧力は今後も強まる見込みだ。

## Document 088: 261006_daiwa_winway

Source PDF: all report pdf/261006_daiwa_winway.pdf
Report source: line報告備份
Date: 2026-10-06
Pages: 4
Extraction status: ok

### Page 1

Summary: Winway reported September revenue of TWD1.25bn (-26.7% MoM; +87.7% 
YoY), brining 3Q26 revenue to a record high of TWD4.56bn (+29.3% QoQ; +152.6% YoY). 
The 3Q26 top-line result beat market consensus by 6.7% but missed Daiwa’s estimate by 
9.1%. Following August’s exceptionally strong top line (TWD1.71bn), we had factored in a 
higher shipment run-rate for September. However, the miss stems primarily from the 
lumpiness of probe-card deliveries (high concentration to a single customer) and customer 
qualification timelines, rather than order cancellations or share loss.

We forecast 4Q26 revenue to track flattish to slightly down QoQ, reflecting lower probe-
card shipment volumes compared to 3Q26, alongside typical year-end pull-in deceleration 
from customers. On profitability, we maintain our view that 2Q26 marked the trough for 
gross margin this year. As the revenue contribution from lower-margin MEMS probe cards 
declines, a more favourable product mix should drive a steady QoQ gross margin recovery, 
which may drive gross margin to reach 40% earlier than market expectations.

Although we remain positive on Winway’s structural growth profile, near-term catalysts 
appear muted. This reflects the revenue lumpiness in probe cards, while additional socket 
capacity will not come online until March 2027—pushing the corresponding revenue 
recognition into 2Q27. We will revisit our financial model upon the company’s 3Q26 results 
release. We currently have a Buy (1) rating on Winway with a 12-month TP of TWD10,000.

周建霖

光環(3234)

在完成客戶 5,000 小時可靠度測試認證後，光環代工業務自 26H2
起重新貢獻營運， 隨稼動率提升及產品組合優化， 本業獲利已顯著
改善並順利轉虧為盈 。 為配合客戶後續產能規劃， 公司亦將斥資約
11 億元進行產線擴充，預期至2027 年底產能規模將達 2026 年底
的 3 倍。此外，針對具 Scale-up 應用潛力的 VCSEL 技術，光環
亦規劃推出 1060nm VCSEL 方案供客戶驗證，有望切入新一代
AI 光互連市場，進一步強化公司中長期競爭優勢。綜合以上，研
究部預估光環 2026 年 EPS 0.57 元，2027 年 EPS 6.77 元，目前
PE 為 26.5x，參考光通上游同業 PE 評價為 20-60x，考量光環本
業獲利將開始由虧轉盈且顯著成長，且也開始佈局 Scale-up 雷射
方案，因此給予 35xPER，目標價 240 元，建議買進。

且產品組合優化下，使整體毛利率回升至雙位數以上，並在費用控

代工事業加速成長，本業由虧轉盈

萬元，M+25.2%，Y+96.9%，7-8 月合計 2.0 億元，Y+103.7%。
代工事業逐月增加片數 ， 研究部認為在稼動率回升及產品組合優化
下，光環獲利將持續獲得顯著改善，其中7 月自結稅後淨利約 900
萬，EPS 0.08 元，反映出本業隨營收的放大而已轉虧為盈。綜合
以 上 ， 研 究 部 預 估 光 環26Q3 營收 3.2 億元， Q+63.9% ，
Y+113.2%，毛利率 33.77%，稅後淨利 4462 萬元，Q 虧轉盈，Y
虧轉盈，EPS 0.35 元。

產能積極布局，同時開發 1060nm VCSEL 自有產品

公告將斥資約 11 億元進行產能擴充及設備擴置。研究部推估，此
次擴產主要係配合客戶 2027～2028 年產能規劃 ， 因而提前建置相

可望轉為穩定獲利，加以產能利用率提升帶動營運槓桿逐步顯現，

結論 
在完成客戶 5,000 小時可靠度測試認證後，光環代工業務自 26H2
起重新貢獻營運，隨稼動率提升及產品組合優化，本業獲利已顯著
改善並順利轉虧為盈。為配合客戶後續產能規劃，公司亦將斥資約
11 億元進行產線擴充， 預期至2027 年底產能規模將達 2026 年底
的 3 倍。此外，針對具 Scale-up 應用潛力的 VCSEL 技術，光環
亦規劃推出 1060nm VCSEL 方案供客戶驗證 ， 有望切入新一代AI
光互連市場，進一步強化公司中長期競爭優勢。綜合以上，研究部

## Document 149: Advanced economies _ Resilience to shock after shock (2026-09-24)

Source PDF: all report pdf/Advanced economies  _ Resilience to shock after shock (2026-09-24).pdf
Report source: BNP Paribas
Date: 2026-09-24
Pages: 3
Extraction status: ok

### Page 1

3
economic-research.bnpparibas.comEcoPerspectives / 3rd Quarter 2026 / September 2026
The bank
for a changing
world
History tends to repeat itself in advanced economies. Once again, growth ultimately fell short of expectations by only a small
margin in the first half of 2026, despite the conflict in Iran. As early as 2025, the impact of tariffs was less severe than feared.
This is a sign that structural factors (notably AI, defence and electrification) are underpinning growth in these countries

and are expected to continue to do so in 2027. However, there are significant risks to the economic outlook, primarily of a
geopolitical nature, with a significant upside risk to inflation and interest rates. The other instance of history repeating itself
concerns a period that those under 20 cannot have experienced: the period leading up to the 2008 crisis, echoes of which can
be seen in terms of growth, inflation, the direction of monetary policy, and long-term interest rates.

Advanced economies are experiencing trends similar to those they
faced nearly 20 years ago:
- Growth remains resilient in 2025-26 despite shocks, as it did in 2006-
07, in the major advanced economies.
- Inflation is above central banks’ targets: as in 2006-07, this calls for a
tightening of monetary policy.
- In Europe and the United States, long-term interest rates have re -
turned to their 2006-07 levels (1996 in Japan): a challenging environ-

ment for public finances in a context where public debt ratios are signi-
ficantly higher than those seen 20 years ago.
THE GLOBAL ECONOMY IN THE MIDST OF A GROWTH CYCLE
We are already well into 2026, and the picture so far is clear: glo -
bal growth is proving resilient. Following a US tariff shock last year,
growth is once again proving, for the time being, to be significantly less
affected by the conflict in Iran than had been anticipated when it first

broke out. Initially, however, there were fears that this new shock would
hit growth even harder. As with the tariff hike in 2025, the impact was
measurable, particularly in terms of consumer confidence. But in both
2025 and 2026, growth has also shown a degree of resilience.
The figures for the second quarter bear this out, despite the sharp rise
in oil prices, the recessionary impact of which is not yet apparent.
According to our forecasts, growth was expected to reach or exceed 1%

in 2026 in the four major advanced economies (the Eurozone, Japan,
the United Kingdom and the United States, where it exceeds 2%) for
the second consecutive year. This had not happened (excluding the
post-COVID catch-up period) since 2006-07. Growth figures are more
moderate (particularly for demographic reasons), but the private sec -
tor is not (at present) showing the same excesses (at that time, the
credit boom had preceded the worst crisis since the 1930s); although

debt levels have recently risen in the US tech sector.
The driving force behind the current growth cycle is clear: artificial
intelligence (AI). It is fuelling global trade and business investment
in most countries. This is clearly the case in the United States, where
business investment is growing strongly as investment plans in the
US tech sector are implemented.
However, the world’s major economies are currently engaged in a
broader push to expand their capital goods investment. Alongside the

ongoing development of services—particularly digital services—we
have seen a rebound in industrial production in most countries since
early 2025. The leading sectors are primarily technology equipment,
machinery, aeronautics, defence and their essential inputs (energy and
metals). This trend is evident everywhere, including in Europe, and
is becoming entrenched over the long term, with an improvement in
business climate indicators, particularly new export orders. Growth is

therefore expected to strengthen further in 2027, particularly in the
Eurozone, where German investment plans are set to gather momen -
tum and whose spillover effects will have a greater impact on the eco-
nomy.
However, not all sectors are experiencing the same momentum. This
is particularly true for the sectors that depend on household consump-
tion, which has shown mixed trends. Our observation that consumer
spending is more buoyant in the United States than in Europe was

confirmed in 2026, and the outlook does not point to a change in this
ranking.
EDITORIAL
RESILIENCE TO SHOCK AFTER SHOCK

### Page 2

4
EcoPerspectives / 3rd Quarter 2026 / September 2026 economic-research.bnpparibas.com
The bank
for a changing
world
While European and US households are experiencing more or less the
same energy price shock, inflation remains higher in the United States.
But this has not prevented consumer spending from keeping pace, pri-
marily because it is driven by the wealthiest households, who are less
sensitive to inflation than to the wealth effect. Furthermore, while the

savings rate remains high in the Eurozone, US households have been
drawing down their savings. This is also true of British households,
which explains why the UK has seen slightly stronger growth since
the beginning of the year. In Japan, government spending has partially
offset energy inflation (while other countries are doing so to a much
lesser extent than in 2022), which has supported consumption growth.
However, these buffers—reduced savings or fiscal support—have their

limits when shocks persist. The two major current conflicts (Iran and
Ukraine) are far from being resolved, and their impact is continuing.
This persistent geopolitical risk and its implications for inflation repre-
sent a significant headwind to the growth outlook for 2027.
HIGHER INFLATION CALLS FOR MONETARY TIGHTENING
Inflation is exceeding the target set by three of the four central banks
examined in this publication, which is another similarity (albeit with

different underlying causes) with the 2006-07 period. Japan is the ex -
ception, as government support measures for households are tempo -
rarily holding back inflation.
Apart from the Bank of Japan, which is engaged in a long-term norma-
lisation process, no central bank was expected to raise its key rates
in 2026. But they are now being prompted to do so for reasons that
vary from one region to another. The first is the conflict in Iran and
the resulting rise in oil prices, which triggered a rebound in energy

inflation from the second quarter onwards. However, since the conflict
was not resolved by the memorandum of understanding signed in June,
and given that the subsequent drop in oil prices proved short-lived,
inflation is expected to rise again. The rise in gas prices is expected to
exacerbate the impact, particularly in Europe, where inventories are
being replenished ahead of winter. Electricity prices are expected to
follow suit in most countries, but at a more moderate pace than in

2022. Indeed, gas is now used less to generate electricity than it was
at that time (thanks to the expansion of renewable energy), and to an
even lesser extent in France.
There is a significant risk that energy inflation will spread further to
other components of inflation in the future, and inflation expectations
could eventually reflect this. This is why the European Central Bank
has already raised its key interest rate twice (in June and September)

and, according to our scenario, will likely do so again in December. For
the same reason, we expect the Bank of England to raise its policy rate
in November. Although the cap on energy prices was maintained for
a long time, it has now been raised. As a result, inflation is expected
to rise between now and the end of the year. The aim will then be to
prevent second-round effects.
Inflation can also be a sign of (too much) economic strength. In the

United States, it has exceeded the 2% target since 2021. The main
argument in favour of a new cycle of rate hikes (following the one in
2022-2023) lies in the current resilience of growth and the improving
labour market. The Federal Reserve (Fed) must balance two equally
important objectives: maximum employment and price stability. Howe-
ver, unlike in the 4 th quarter of 2025, when the decline in job crea -
tion justified monetary easing (by 75 basis points), the current sharp

rebound in job creation completely reverses that analysis. According
to our forecasts, the unemployment rate is expected to decrease in
the coming months, which pushes back the prospect of a decline in
core inflation. This situation persists due to both supply constraints
(over which a central bank has no control) and excess demand (which
the Fed can, however, mitigate). Consequently, the Fed raised its key
rate in September and is expected to implement two further rate hikes

between now and January 2027.
In Japan, finally, while headline inflation has been brought down by
government support measures, core inflation remains significant.
Wage pressures are mounting against a backdrop of labour shortages.
The Bank of Japan is therefore being forced to accelerate the pace of its
monetary normalisation.
In all these regions, inflation is currently expected to peak in Q4 2026
or Q1 2027. It is against this backdrop of temporary inflation that rate

hikes could also come to an end by that time (excluding Japan). Howe-
ver, this assumes that once the energy shock—the course of which
remains uncertain—subsides, inflation will fall. The effects of El Niño,
which are still difficult to gauge at present, could take over. Inflation
would then remain above the central banks’ target for a longer period.
Under this scenario, rate hikes would go further than is currently en -
visaged.
THE RISE IN LONG- TERM INTEREST RATES: A FACTOR THAT WILL WEIGH

ON PUBLIC FINANCES
Monetary policies have also to offset the inflationary effects of fiscal
policy, particularly in the United States (where the budget deficit is
high and shows no signs of shrinking) and in Japan (where the new
government is pursuing pro-growth policies). In Europe, fiscal policy
is playing a much smaller role than it did in 2022. While the stimulus
measures implemented at that time did help reduce the inflation rate
in the short term (by nearly 2.5 pp in France, for example), by keeping

demand at a high level, they delayed disinflation and increased the
scale of the monetary tightening required.
The outbreak of the conflict in Iran acted as a catalyst: it triggered a
rise in long-term rates (which had been trending downwards at the
start of the year) to levels close to those of 2006-07. This rise is wides-
pread, but its causes vary. Inflation expectations appear to play a more
significant role in the Eurozone, whereas the rise in real interest rates

seems to be the main factor in the United States and Japan.

EcoPerspectives / 3rd Quarter 2026 / September 2026 economic-research.bnpparibas.com
The bank
for a changing
world
Higher inflation and more resilient real growth than feared point in
the same direction: stronger than expected nominal growth that is li -
kely to be sustained. However, in the long term, high nominal growth
tends to drive up nominal interest rates (as it is a positive sign of more
profitable investment projects), in contrast to the problem of secular

stagnation raised in the 2010s (which combined low nominal growth
with low nominal interest rates).
The other issue is the high level of fiscal deficits (too high for a period
of growth), with various situations. In several countries, the public
debt-to-GDP ratio continues to rise. In the United States, deficit reduc-
tion does not appear to be a priority, to the extent that it is expected
to remain above 6% of GDP at the very least until the end of Donald

Trump’s term of office (with little hope that the mid-term elections will
significantly alter this trend). In the United Kingdom and France, fiscal
consolidation has begun, but it is being hampered by the simultaneous
increase in certain areas of expenditure (debt servicing, defence and
healthcare, due in particular to an ageing population), which is slowing
the pace of consolidation. In Germany, the implementation of invest -
ment plans is leading to a widening of the deficit. While this does not

pose a sustainability problem, it does result in an increase in annual
debt issuance. In Japan, the budget deficit is kept in check by interest
payments that remain low, as the rise in interest rates is recent and
the average maturity of the debt is high (10 years). However, expansio-
nary fiscal policy could pose further problems in terms of debt dyna -
mics if it were to continue through the end of the decade. In Italy and
Spain, fiscal consolidation has been sufficient to stabilise the public

debt-to-GDP ratio (and even to reduce it in Spain), but the high level of
Italy’s debt service it vulnerable to a rise in interest rates.
In all these countries, the fiscal arithmetic is simpler today than it
will be in 2030. Indeed, in most cases, only half of the debt was is -
sued after 2022 (that is, after the end of the zero-interest-rate period).
The debt service has so far only partially risen, except in the United
States, where most of the increase has already occurred due to a shor-

ter average debt maturity (6 years). This means that, for all countries
combined, the rate of GDP growth remains higher than the average
interest rate paid on public debt (or the effective interest rate).
By 2030, the effective rate will have risen and caught up with
GDP growth. As a result, in most cases, the debt service will have re -
turned to the level as a share of GDP seen in the early 2000s. In order to
stabilise public debt-to-GDP ratios, primary balances will need to have

improved. This will even require the generation of primary surpluses,
particularly in Italy (which is already achieving this), France and the
United Kingdom. This is a crucial shift that governments will need to
introduce as early as the 2027 budget; otherwise, they risk postponing
the stabilisation of their public debt-to-GDP ratio once again.
Article completed on 17 September 2026
Stéphane Colliac
stephane.colliac@bnpparibas.com

2
WESTPAC ECONOMICS
BULLETIN
The Cotality home value index, covering all of Australia,
fell 1.1% in September with downward revisions to the
previous two months again shaving a further 0.4% off prices.
The correction has now seen prices decline 5.2% from their

March peak. Annual growth is flat.
The price correction remains broad, Cotality reporting 97% of
capital city suburbs experienced falls over the three months
to September, Darwin the only city to record gains. Sydney
and Melbourne continue to see more pronounced declines,
albeit moderating a touch, particularly in Melbourne.
Against this, declines in Brisbane, Adelaide and Perth have
accelerated over the last few months. Across segments, unit
prices and lower price tiers continue to track slightly milder

falls than houses and top tiers.
Seasonal variations accentuated price weakness through winter
but are now neutral. The peak-to-trough price decline nationally
is slightly milder at 4.6% in seasonally adjusted terms. Unless
specified, figures in titles, tables and the commentary below are
in non-seasonally adjusted terms, consistent with the ‘headline’
figures reported by Cotality. However, figures shown in charts
are in seasonally adjusted terms.

Preliminary estimates of turnover show rolling 3mth volumes
down 3.9%qtr in September and 17.4%yr with previous
estimates revised 1-2ppts lower. The monthly pattern
suggests small gains in July and August have given way to
another sizeable fall in September.
Looking at the price performance across different jurisdictions:
• Sydney prices dropped 1.4% fall in September, monthly
declines averaging 1.6% over the previous four months,
tracking a 17% annualised pace of decline since May. All

segments and tiers recorded falls in Q3 but with top and
middle tier houses notably weaker.
• Melbourne prices fell 0.7%mth, a slower pace than other
capitals. This likely reflects the weaker starting point coming
into the correction, prices already back to the levels seen in
mid-2021. As elsewhere, 'top tier' houses prices are leading
the decline. 'Low tier' unit prices mostly held their ground in
Q3, prices in this sub-segment already plumbing 10yr lows.

• Brisbane was the weakest capital city price-wise, recording
a 1.5% drop in September with previous monthly declines
also revised down heavily (August from 1% to 1.6% and
July from 1.2% to 1.7%). The combined effect has taken
the rolling 3mth change to –4.7%qtr from –2.7%qtr last
month and –0.6%qtr in July. At 5.9%yr, annual growth is
still positive but losing altitude quickly, having run at 18%yr
as recently as May. Sellers have been slow to respond to

the sudden stop with total listings up over 50% vs a year
ago, albeit still not high compared to long run averages
(reflecting the extremely tight starting point for the Brisbane
market).
• Adelaide dwelling prices fell 1.3%mth, the August decline

also marked down from 0.8% to 1.1%, annual price growth
dropping to 6.5%yr. The price correction began later in
Adelaide but is now running at a similar pace to prices
nationally.
• Perth recorded a 1.2% decline but also saw big downward
revisions to previous months (August from 0.8% to –1.7%
and July from –1.3% to –1.8%). At 10.1%yr, annual growth
is still solidly positive but was tracking at over 25%yr earlier
in the year. As with Adelaide and Brisbane, the detail shows

a more even spread of declines across price tiers. However,
turnover is showing a milder pull-back in Adelaide.
• The smaller capitals were again more mixed, price growth
still positive in Darwin (0.4%mth, 11.9%yr), relatively
resilient in Hobart (–0.5%mth, 7%yr) but weaker in
Canberra (–1.1%mth, –1.6%yr).
• Regional markets showed a 0.9% decline in Qld but slightly
milder declines in NSW (–0.7%) and Victoria (0.3%). Annual
prices growth is in the 3.4-6.3%yr range.

Our recently released Housing Pulse report (here) provides
a deeper diver into the situation in Australia's housing
markets. While the absence of selling pressure, still tight
supply-demand balance and sentiment mix suggests the
correction was still relatively 'orderly' through the September
quarter, the latest monthly data shows momentum remains
unambiguously negative.
That said, there are clearly more headwinds to come. The
RBA latest interest rate rise and what now looks to be a

more likely prospect of a follow-up increase at its meeting in
early November will impact in coming months. Next week's
consumer sentiment update will show how the change, and
the latest round of weak updates, is shaping housing-related
sentiment. Either way, momentum looks set to remain weak
into year-end and, barring a significantly improved inflation
and interest rate outlook, early 2027.

### Page 3

Source: Eurostat, *forecasts ING Research
Following ﬁve consecutive years of contraction, Germany’s
construction sector is expected to grow again in 2026. Europe’s
largest construction market shrank by more than 10% between
2020 and 2025. We expect a modest recovery this year, with
growth gradually gaining momentum in 2027, supported by
housing and infrastructure.
In 2025, France’s construction output fell by 3.5%, followed by
quarter-on-quarter declines of 1.2% and 0.9% in the ﬁrst two

quarters of 2026. French sector conﬁdence weakened further, while
housing permits in August remained below their level a year earlier.
Combined with sluggish GDP growth, this points to a 2.5%
contraction in 2026.
Spain’s construction sector is still recovering. After losing more than
25% of its output between 2019 and 2022, the market began to
rebound in 2023. We forecast growth of 3% in 2026. Building
permits have risen sharply in recent years, although momentum is

easing. Contractor conﬁdence is easing from a very high level in
recent months. Even so, robust GDP growth should support
construction this year and next.
For the Netherlands, we expect construction output to stabilise in
2026 after a weak ﬁrst half, followed by modest growth in 2027.

Housing construction should gradually recover as the recent rise in
building permits feeds through into starts and completions,
although projects are often delayed and the recovery will remain
limited. Infrastructure remains an important driver.
Poland’s construction sector had a volatile start to 2026, with
output falling by 6.8% in the ﬁrst quarter due to the cold winter
before rebounding by 8% in the second. Building permits are rising
slightly, conﬁdence remains stable and robust GDP growth of 3.4%

should support demand. The resumption of EU funding is also
expected to support infrastructure investment. Nevertheless, we
expect construction output to remain ﬂat in 2026, largely because
of the weak ﬁrst quarter, followed by modest growth of 2% in 2027.
Input prices are marginally increasing
Higher energy prices are pushing up production costs for energy-
intensive materials such as concrete, cement and bricks. Even so,
material prices are generally rising only gradually. In September,

13% of EU producers planned to raise selling prices over the next
three months, compared with 11% in January, before the Iran war.
Passing higher input costs through the value chain is also
becoming harder. Governments, non-residential investors and
homebuyers already face higher interest rates, making further cost
increases more diﬃcult to absorb.
Contract terms may also pose a risk for contractors. Public price-
adjustment schemes are often too limited to absorb shocks, while

countries without them, such as Spain, face greater exposure.
Private contracts oﬀer even less protection, increasing the risk of
disputes, delays and halted projects.

### Page 5

Source: Eurostat, ING Research
Low water levels are delaying deliveries
Low water levels are creating upward cost pressure on building
materials. Many raw materials used in building materials, such as
sand, gravel and chemicals, are transported by inland waterways.
When river levels are low, barges can carry less cargo, which
increases transport costs and delays deliveries in the construction
supply chain. The impact has remained limited but is slowly

increasing. In September, 8% of EU construction companies
reported production constraints due to material shortages. This is
above the 5.7% in January and the 6.4% recorded during the low-
water period in September 2018.
Proﬁt conﬁdence rebounds, but energy risks return
Higher energy prices and the prospect of rising building-material
costs initially alarmed construction companies, which feared a
sharp hit to proﬁt margins in the ﬁrst quarter of 2026. As the Iran

war continued, however, gas prices rose less dramatically than
during the 2022 energy crisis. Conﬁdence recovered, and second-
quarter proﬁt expectations exceeded pre-war levels. This rebound
may prove excessive, as energy prices have since begun rising
again.

### Page 6

Source: RICS, ING Research
Residential growth stalls
Between summer 2024 and the end of 2025, the number of
building permits issued in the EU rose by approximately 15%. Since
then, however, permit issuance has broadly stabilised, suggesting
that momentum has faded in recent months. Several factors may
explain this development:
Higher interest rates make homes less aﬀordable, particularly
new builds, because buyers often have to ﬁnance payments
during construction.

The conﬂict in the Middle East has heightened geopolitical
uncertainty. This may make buyers more reluctant to purchase
a home, especially a new build, because completion can take
up to two years. The longer waiting period increases
uncertainty compared with buying an existing home.
Higher energy prices have increased the cost of energy-
intensive building materials, although the rise in overall
construction costs has so far remained modest.
One possible oﬀset is that buyers may prefer new homes because

they are generally more energy-eﬃcient than existing properties.
However, the stabilisation in permit issuance suggests that this
advantage is not strong enough to drive further growth.
The number of permits issued for new houses is an important
indicator of future volumes in the residential sector. Typically, it
takes between one and two years from the granting of a permit to
the completion of a new home.

EU Building permits new dwellings, SA (index 2021=100
Germany follows the broader European recovery after a particularly
sharp fall in housing permits in 2021 and 2022. The government’s
‘housing construction turbo’ (Bau-Turbo) aims to speed up
planning and approvals in high-demand areas. Its impact, however,
will depend on local authorities, which may hesitate over loss of
control and legal uncertainty.
The permit data for France at the beginning of the year looked

promising. However, the second quarter was disappointing. France
expanded its zero-interest mortgage scheme (PTZ: Prêt à Taux Zéro)
for new-build homes in April 2025 to support ﬁrst-time buyers and
boost housing construction, but the building permit data suggests
the policy has yet to gain traction.
Dutch permits for new homes surged in the second quarter of 2026.
Statistics Netherlands has since paused publication of recent data,
saying the increase appears implausibly large and may reﬂect

legislative changes related to grid congestion. Limited grid capacity
continues to delay new connections and construction projects,
while nitrogen restrictions cause further setbacks. The
government's plan to reduce nitrogen emissions of peak polluters
and make way for other business activities is now under
parliamentary review.
Spanish housing permits nearly tripled between 2020 and 2024,
largely reﬂecting a rebound from the exceptionally low pre-

pandemic era base and a recovery in housing demand. Growth now
appears to have peaked, with permits falling by almost 2% in the

ﬁnanced through European recovery funds, aims to speed up and

Business case still diﬃcult
Housing shortages remain severe in many European cities, but new

housing projects are more diﬃcult to make commercially viable in
some countries than in others. Although permit issuance has
increased in many markets, construction costs remain high.
Builders and developers often cannot fully pass these costs on to
buyers, especially where existing homes are still cheaper than a few
years ago. As buyers can choose between new and existing homes,
relatively higher new-build prices are likely to push many towards
existing properties.

This is particularly true in France, Germany and Austria, where
existing-home prices have risen less than the European average. In
France, they remain even below their levels three years ago.
Builders and developers therefore have very limited (or no) scope to
pass on higher material costs, leaving many projects unproﬁtable
at current market prices. As a result, they are postponing
construction until conditions improve.

Infrastructure growth continues
EU infrastructure output grew by 2.2% in 2025. Growth slowed in

relatively cold, wet winter across many European regions
temporarily disrupted activity at infrastructure sites, though the
sector recovered some of the lost ground in the following months.

Railway construction is showing strong growth. Across the EU,
activity is being supported by both network renewals and major

cross-border projects. Examples include Rail Baltica, which will
connect the Baltic States with Poland, the Lyon–Turin rail link
between France and Italy and high-speed rail investment in Spain,
Portugal and Italy. Together, these projects illustrate the EU’s drive
to expand rail capacity.
Telecommunications construction has grown modestly in recent
years, supported by ﬁbre-optic expansion and continued
investment in 5G networks across the EU. Although coverage is

improving, progress varies widely between member states and
signiﬁcant investment is still needed to extend high-capacity
networks, particularly in rural and other lagging areas.
Roadworks, which account for around 30% of EU infrastructure
output, remain under pressure as many local and regional
authorities face budget constraints. Across the EU, public funding is
competing with other investment and spending priorities, limiting
the resources available for road construction and maintenance.

Overall, Europe’s construction recovery will remain slow and uneven.
Infrastructure investment and a gradual German recovery will
provide support, but high ﬁnancing costs, weak housing economics
and geopolitical risks will limit momentum. For now, infrastructure
will do most of the heavy lifting within the sector.

## Document 243: France_ double-digit growth in business creations, a slowdown in the rise in bankruptcies and stable non-performing loans ratio (2026-10-08)

Source PDF: all report pdf/France_ double-digit growth in business creations, a slowdown in the rise in bankruptcies and stable non-performing loans ratio (2026-10-08).pdf
Report source: BNP Paribas
Date: 2026-10-08
Pages: 1
Extraction status: ok

### Page 1

Paris, 8 October 2026

EcoFlash

France: double-digit growth in business creations, a slowdown in the rise in bankruptcies and 
stable non-performing loans ratio

Business creations rose by 12% y/y over the first eight months of 2026 1. This is significantly higher than the 5.3% y/y 
growth recorded in 2025. This increase was driven by sectors linked to information and communication technologies.

Meanwhile, business bankruptcies rose by 5.3% y/y between January and July, according to data from the Banque de 
France (compared with the 3.6% y/y increase for the whole of 2025). The rise was more pronounced in the 1st quarter (+8% y/y) 
before slowing down. This slowdown could persist, as, according to Altares, payment delays have fallen to an average of 12.3 
days in the second quarter of 2026, compared with 14.1 days a year earlier (and 13.1 days for the European average).

Rising bankruptcies are more heavily affecting the services sector, which is also experiencing slightly stronger growth 
in business creations. This correlation is particularly noticeable in the retail sector, as business creations have risen by 13% 
since the start of 2026, while bankruptcies are up by 3%. In construction and manufacturing, business bankruptcies have been 
falling since the second quarter of 2026.

The average ratio of non -performing corporate loans has remained stable at a moderately high level for the past year  
(4.0% of total loans and advances in Q2 2026). In construction, where activity is more cyclical than in other sectors, the ra tio of 
non-performing loans has reached its highest level since 2019, standing at 9.5%.

The proportion of loans that have deteriorated but are not yet non-performing (“Stage 2” according to IFRS 9 terminology) 
is stable, standing at 15.4% q/q, and remains below its all -time high (16.1%). This indicator, which helps to anticipate trends in 
non-performing loans, is relatively encouraging.

Despite a significant increase since 2021, the economic weight of bankruptcies remains, on average, below its peak in 
Q1 2025 (0.53 compared with 0.61, respectively). This indicator generally allows bankruptcies to be weighted based on the size 
of the businesses. However, the economic weight of bankrupted small businesses is at a historically high level (2.11).

Take a look at our EcoCharts on trends in business creations and bankruptcies in France and the 
associated risks. It will be updated on a quarterly basis.

Thomas Humblot 
Economiste, team: Banking economics 
thomas.humblot@bnpparibas.com

Stéphane Colliac 
Head of Advanced economies, France 
stephane.colliac@bnpparibas.com

1 For more details, see Business creations and bankruptcies in France: double-digit growth in business creations, lower growth in bankruptcies, 
and stable non-performing loans, 8 October 2026.

## Document 281: new era of chip testing 20261008 ubs

Source PDF: all report pdf/new era of chip testing 20261008 ubs.pdf
Report source: line報告備份
Date: 2026-10-08
Pages: 31
Extraction status: ok

### Page 1

ab 8 October 2026
Global Research
Asia Semiconductor Insight
AI put to the test: the new era of chip testing
Time to revisit semiconductor testing as content growth accelerates
Our core thesis is that AI-driven chip complexity is drastically increasing testing content
across wafer, package, and system levels, creating a multi-year growth opportunity that
the market has yet to fully price in. Testing companies in Taiwan have underperformed
TAIEX in the past three months (indexing at -9% vs TAIEX +7%), yet to recover from the

July market deleveraging. Investor hesitation currently stems from 1) fear of test content
growth expectations already priced-in, 2) lingering concerns over shorter test times, and
3) near-term capacity constraints among major suppliers of probe cards, load boards,
socket pins, testers, and handlers, caused by heightened testing demand. Ultimately,
these concerns overshadow the structural increase in test insertions required by AI
accelerators, chiplets, and advanced packaging. As advanced packaging scales, testing

intensity must rise in tandem, driving growth through higher content per device rather
than semiconductor unit growth alone.
Expanding testing opportunities with shift-left for CP and increasing FT content
Chip probing (CP) supply chain should be the key beneficiary of the industry's shift-left
testing trend, as more test insertions move to the wafer level before packaging, in
addition to longer CP time. We believe investors underestimate this trend, which should

accelerate with broader adoption of 2.5D/3D packaging, chiplets and larger AI
packages, driving demand for high pin count probe cards, probers, and thermal-control
equipment. We expect strong growth for Hermes Testing (HTSI)  as thermal-
management requirements rise with increasing chip power and complexity, with prober
thermal-control module shipments reaching 3,000/4,100 units in 2027E/28E, up from
1,200 in 2026E . We forecast Chunghwa Precision Test (CHPT) 's sales to exceed

consensus by 7%/15% in 2027E/28E, supported by structural AI/HPC testing demand
and rising value-added content. Importantly, shift-left testing does not diminish the role
of final test (FT) and mission-mode testing. Test content and test time should continue to
increase as validation becomes increasingly critical for complex, large-form-factor AI
accelerators. We expect larger package sizes and rising system-level testing
requirements to drive demand for WinWay's high-pin-count sockets, supporting

74%/71% YoY sales growth in 2027E/28E.
Future of testing: co-packaged optics (CPO) will be a major opportunity
CPO testing represents a significant new market opportunity, though we expect the
ecosystem to remain fragmented rather than consolidating around a single winner. As AI
scaling shifts the industry bottleneck from memory to interconnect, CPO adoption is
becoming increasingly important, creating new testing challenges that will require
collaboration across equipment, optics, and test solution providers. Leveraging

ficonTEC's optical alignment expertise and HTSI's probe, testing, and manufacturing
capabilities, we estimate the HTSI  will deliver 40 units of insertion 2 equipment and 70
units of insertion 3 testing equipment in 2027 , with further upside in 2028 as CPO
deployment expands from scale-out towards scale-up.
Initiate Hermes Testing, Chunghwa Precision Test, WinWay with Buy ratings
We initiate coverage on semiconductor testing solution provider Hermes Testing with

a Buy rating,  NT$8,880 price target based on 31x 2028E PE, for thermal control/CPO
with the most attractive risk/reward in our testing coverage; test interface PCB supplier
CHPT with a Buy rating, NT$5,000 price target based on 31x avg 2027-28E PE, for
CPU/TPU programs and test board content gain; and semiconductor test socket leader
WinWay with a Buy rating, NT$8,900 price target based on 30x avg 2027-28E PE,
for socket content growth and margin recovery. Among our testing coverage, we also

including information on the Quantitative Research Review published by UBS, begin on page 22.

Q: Is chip probing becoming increasingly important in the testing supply chain?
Yes.   As package costs rise, foundries must improve known-good-die (KGD) screening before
packaging, increasing demand for wafer-level testing. This should drive demand for probe cards,

probers and advanced thermal control solutions capable of managing higher power consumption,
wafer warpage and tighter test tolerances. We forecast HTSI/CHPT/WinWay' probe card business to
see a 31%/35%/33% sales CAGR for 2026-2031E, as they are positioned to grow strongly given
their smaller scale and robust growth drivers from shift left, CPU, AI accelerators. We forecast HTSI to
ship 1,200/3,000/4,100 thermal control modules in 2026E/27E/28E, as advanced wafer-level test for

AI accelerators will require thermal control solutions in order to prevent probe card needle burnt.
Q: Will FT and SLT content continue to grow in the coming years?
Yes. Despite concerns that final-test (FT) time could flatten or decline with Nvidia's Rubin GPUs, we
expect overall FT content to continue growing as advanced packaging complexity and chip sizes
increase. We also expect broader adoption of system-level testing (SLT)/mission-mode testing, as AI

accelerators require increasingly stringent validation to ensure optimal system and rack-level
performance. We forecast WinWay's socket business to see a 33% sales CAGR for 2026-2031E.
Q: Will CPO testing be a revenue generator in 2027-28 to supply chain players?
Yes. Testing remains a key bottleneck, creating a new equipment opportunity beginning in 2027E.
We expect initial CPO volumes in 2027, followed by broader adoption in 2028. Testing remains one

of the key bottlenecks to commercialization, driving demand for new equipment and test solutions.
We believe early leaders such as ficonTEC (partnership with HTSI) are well positioned to benefit from
the initial ramp, alongside suppliers including Chroma ATE and Hon Precision.
1) We believe upside from agentic AI CPU demand is not reflected in CHPT and WinWay shares. We
estimate CPU-related demand could contribute 20-30% of 2027E sales  for CHPT and WinWay,

respectively, with further upside in 2028E. 2) We believe market expectations for 2027 CPO testing
equipment shipments remain conservative, particularly for insertion 2 equipment. Based on our
industry analysis, we estimate HTSI will deliver 40 insertion 2 and 70 insertion 3 systems in 2027E, this
should be higher than the market's expectation of no insertion 2 and limited insertion 3 shipment
due to engineering bottleneck. We also see further upside in 2028E as CPO adoption expands.

Testing is becoming one of the largest incremental beneficiaries of AI-driven semiconductor
complexity. As more test insertions are required across wafer, package and system levels, testing
content per device should increase materially, expanding the industry's addressable market and
supporting multi-year growth across the supply chain.
TSMC and OSATs have been expanding their CoWoS capacities aggressively, from 40kwpm in 2024
to around 90 kwpm in 2025, and tracking to expand CoWoS capacity toward 160kwpm  in 2026E.

TSMC also ramped up expanding N3/N2 capacity, with multiple product launches from customers'
pipelines—Nvidia's Rubin, AMD's MI400 series/Venice CPU and other cloud ASICs—translating into
foreseeable, strong testing demand.

Shift-left igniting secular growth for chip probing
With the industry increasingly adopting shift-left testing, chip probing (CP) and its supply
chain should be key beneficiaries of a multi-year structural growth trend. This underpins

our above-consensus forecasts for Chunghwa Precision Test (CHPT), with 2027E/28E
sales 7%/15% above consensus driven by stronger AI/HPC demand, capacity expansion,
and rising value-added content. WinWay should also benefit through its partnership
with Technoprobe, with probe card revenue growth remaining robust at 94%/72% in
2026E/27E following 161%/260% growth in 2024/25.
Hermes Testing (HTSI) should be another major beneficiary as increasing chip power

and complexity drive demand for advanced thermal-management modules used
alongside high-end probing systems. With TEL's leading AI-oriented probers typically
paired with advanced thermal-control solutions, we expect HTSI's thermal-module
shipments to reach 3,000 units in 2027E from 1,200 in 2026E. Combined with testing,
installation and service revenue, this should drive HTSI sales growth of 127%/152% in
2026E/27E.
Accelerators still in focus with TPU as another significant growth driver

Nvidia remains a key growth driver for both CHPT and WinWay. We expect both
companies to gain share with Rubin, with WinWay's socket share improving from
roughly 50% historically to 60% with Rubin, while CHPT is positioned to supply around
70% of probe card and final test PCB content. Nvidia currently accounts for roughly
50% of WinWay's revenue and 30% of CHPT's.
Beyond Nvidia, we view Google's TPU roadmap as a meaningful incremental
opportunity. Following the successful ramp of TPU v8t with MediaTek as design partner,

CHPT is positioned to supply probe card PCBs, final test load boards, system-level test
boards and burn-in boards. WinWay will supply sockets for both TPU v8t and v9, with
the larger v9 package providing another growth leg. We estimate production of 4mn
TPU v8t units in 2027E and 2.3mn TPU v9 packages (9.2mn dies) alongside 2m v8t units
in 2028E.
CPU business as a supplemental catalyst for test interface in an agentic
world
With agentic AI proliferation, CPU demand is increasing significantly and this directly

correlates to increasing demand for testing consumables. WinWay and CHPT should
both benefit from the strong ramp up of Nvidia's ARM based Vera CPU and Google's
ARM based Axion CPU in 2026E and 2027E. WinWay will be supplying sockets and
probe cards for Vera and Axion, and CHPT will be the probe card and PCB supplier for
both Vera and Axion. WinWay will have the additional upside from AMD's x86 Venice
CPU, and CHPT could capture some share through AMD's equipment vendor

procurement. We estimate CHPT's CPU related sales to be low to mid-teens % of total
sales in 2026E and growing to 20-30% in 2027E  with CHPT's share expansions with
turnkey probe card solutions for Google's Axion. For WinWay, we believe CPU should
account for around 30% of sales in 2026E/27E.

### Page 4

Asia Semiconductor Insight 8 October 2026 ab 4
CPO testing fuelling future growth for semiconductor testing
ficonTEC's 25 years of precision optical-test expertise makes it an early leader in CPO test
equipment, particularly in optical alignment, a critical capability for high-volume
manufacturing. Through its partnership with ficonTEC, HTSI is well positioned to
benefit from the initial CPO ramp in 2027E-28E by supporting manufacturing scale-up.

We expect both insertion 2 and insertion 3 testing to be required over the next two
years. Despite the technical challenges of double-sided wafer probing, insertion 2
testing remains important given current optical-engine yields and the need to identify
known-good optical engines before fibre-array-unit (FAU) coupling. This becomes
increasingly critical as switch designs integrate larger numbers of optical engines.
We estimate HTSI will ship 40 insertion 2 and 70 insertion 3 systems in 2027E , with

further upside in 2028E as CPO adoption expands and next-generation platforms move
into volume production.
Testing TAM expanding with IC complexity: leaders likely to
outperform industry growth
Our central view is that testing content per AI accelerator is growing faster than
semiconductor unit volumes, creating a multi-year expansion in testing TAM. We believe
semiconductor testing is entering a period of structural growth as AI-driven chip
complexity increases testing intensity across wafer, package and system levels. The

industry's shift-left strategy, which moves more testing upstream in the semiconductor
manufacturing process, is resulting in longer and more frequent test insertions. As cloud
AI GPUs and ASICs become increasingly complex, additional testing is required to ensure
functionality, performance and yield, while reducing defective parts per million (DPPM).
More robust testing should improve deployment efficiency and lower the cost of
operating AI infrastructure.

Semiconductor testing generally comprises four stages: wafer/chip probing (CP), final
test (FT), system-level test (SLT), and burn-in. CP is conducted at the wafer-level before
packaging, while FT, SLT, and burn-in occur after packaging. The below Figure 3S e m i c o n d u c t o r  t e s t i n g  p r o c e s s :  f r o m  w a f e r  l e v e l  t o  p a c k a g e  l e v e l
illustrates the semiconductor manufacturing flow.

We estimate semiconductor testing historically accounts for ~1% of total
semiconductor revenue. Within testing, consumables such as probe cards, sockets and
interface boards represent ~10-15% of industry revenue. According to Yole, the probe
card, socket and interface board markets should grow from US$3.8bn, US$2.3bn and
US$1.7bn in 2026 to US$5.5bn, US$3.1bn and US$2.0bn by 2031, respectively.

Nonetheless, leaders within the testing sector are likely to stand out among competition
with greater sales CAGR comparing to the industry. Below we highlight Hermes

drivers and how this compares with broader industry sales CAGR.
We see HTSI's sales for the next few years to mainly  be driven by 1) thermal control
module shipment and 2) CPO equipment shipment. In the past, thermal control

modules weren't necessary for chip probing, due to less TDP for the chips. However, as
new iterations of AI accelerators now have substantial power draw, this unlocks a brand
new TAM for HTSI to enter with their thermal control modules to pair with probers, in
order to prevent probe card damage.
 Robust thermal control module shipments.  We estimate HTSI to ship
3,000/4,100 thermal control modules in 2027E/28E, with continuous demand in
future years,  bringing their 2026-31E semiconductor equipment sales CAGR to

48%.
 Thermal control module ASP expansion.  Gen 2 thermal control modules
should start to ship in 2027E which will drive tool ASP up around 25-50%.
 Demand supported by Advantest expansions. We believe the 3,000 thermal
control modules shipment forecast for 2027E is not far-fetched, as we read-across
from Advantest's announced capacity expansion plan (testers will be paired with
probers with a 1:1 ratio) with increasing capacity up to 5,000 testers within

FY2027/3 and they also announced a 10,000-unit target within CY2028.
Although the testers capacity expansion plan includes testers for final test, we do
see a good portion to be allocated to chip probing given the shift-left testing trend
increasing both testing insertions and testing time before chip packaging.
 CPO is another brand new TAM for semiconductor testing.  As insertion 2
and 3 testing equipment demand starts to rise in 2027E with scale-out CPO

adoption, we expect HTSI to ship 40/70 insertion 2 and 3 equipment in 2027E ,
with this number growing in 2028E to 80/160 and higher in future years as CPO
proliferates, and driving 52% 2026-31E test equipment & engineering services
sales CAGR.
 CPO to see content increase. Gen 2 insertion 3 equipment may start to ship in
2027E, with higher throughput and tool ASP .
 Insertion 2 might scale down, but not likely  any time soon.  Insertion 2
equipment shipment might come down in 2029E and beyond, as foundries scale-

up yield for photonic IC (PIC) in order to lessen the time consuming insertion 2
testing burden.

The AI era has materially increased the value of semiconductor testing. Compared with
traditional mobile and automotive chips, AI accelerators incorporate larger compute
dies, more advanced nodes, chiplet architectures, advanced packaging technologies,
multiple I/O dies, and increasing HBM content. These factors drive higher-value test

consumables, including advanced MEMS probe cards, multilayer interface boards, and
next-generation sockets designed to support higher pin counts, thermal loading, and
power delivery requirements.
New iterations of semiconductors, especially for AI / HPC applications, are set to increase
in both front-end wafer fabrication advancements and back-end packaging
complexities with each iterations (i.e. Nvidia's roadmap from Blackwell to Rubin to
Feynman or Google's TPU roadmap from v7 to v8 to v9).   This trend is evident with

TSMC's front-end  and TSMC, Intel, and OSAT's back-end advanced packaging capacity
expansions. For front-end, we see industry N3 capacity expanding from 156kwpm in
year end 2025 to 224/283/293kpwm in year end 2026E/27E/28E, and N2 capacity from
47kwpm in year-end 2025 to 111/200/312kpwm in year end 2026E/27E/28E. For back-
end, we are seeing industry CoWoS capacity rising from 90kwpm in 2025 to 160kwpm
in 2026E and further expanding to 270kwpm in 2027E.

accelerators become larger, more power-hungry and more complex. Rising thermal

design power and packaging complexity increase validation requirements, driving
longer test times and higher demand for test equipment to prevent testing from
becoming a production bottleneck.

While architectural changes can affect test requirements for individual product
generations, we expect the long-term trend of rising testing content to remain intact.
For example, Nvidia's Rubin Ultra adopts a simpler package structure than previously
expected, from a 4-die structure to a 2-die structure and Rubin Ultra HBM de-spec from

HBM4E 12-Hi to initially use HBM4 8-Hi and upgrade to HBM4E 8-Hi at the latter stages,
which may moderate near-term test growth. However, future products such as Nvidia's
Feynman are expected to reintroduce greater package complexity, with a four-GPU per
package structure. More importantly, any reduction in test time can be offset by higher
production volumes, making throughput requirements equally important for testing
demand.

Below we summarize key technical items of probe cards, sockets, and load boards/PCB
used for semiconductor testing:
Probe cards and chip probing (CP)
Probe cards are used during chip probing (CP), the wafer-level stage of semiconductor

testing that identifies known-good dies (KGD) before packaging. As advanced
packaging technologies such as CoWoS, 3D stacking and hybrid bonding become more
prevalent, CP is becoming increasingly important because a single defective die or HBM
stack can compromise the entire package. This is driving the industry's shift-left testing
strategy, with more testing conducted earlier in the manufacturing process to identify
defects before costly packaging steps.

Probe cards consist of contact elements (probes) mounted on a PCB and are used
together with probers and automated test equipment (ATE). During testing, wafers are
placed on the prober chuck while the probe card makes electrical contact with individual
dies. The tester then performs electrical, parametric, and functional tests to determine
whether each die meets performance requirements. Dies that fail testing are removed
from the production flow prior to dicing and packaging.

Furthermore, probe cards are highly customised products designed for specific chip
architectures and testing platforms. As AI accelerators adopt larger dies, higher pin
counts and more advanced packaging technologies, probe card complexity and content
value continue to increase.

### Page 9

Asia Semiconductor Insight 8 October 2026 ab 9

cards to find known-good dies (KGD) and
eliminate faulty dies

The global probe card market remains concentrated among a handful of suppliers:
 FormFactor (FORM.O): World's largest probe card company (including both logic
and memory probe cards) by revenue as of year end 2025. Key supplier for HBM

probe cards.
 Technoprobe (TPRO.MI): World's largest logic probe card company by revenue
as of year end 2025. Key supplier for AI accelerators MEMS probe cards, especially
for GPU.
 MJC (6871.T): Japanese company, mainly specializing in memory probe cards, 3rd
largest probe card company by revenue as of year end 2025.
 MPI (6223.TWO) : Traditionally specializes in cantilever  probe cards, now
expanding its MEMS probe capacity. 4th largest probe card company by revenue as

of year end 2025. A probe card supplier of AI ASICs.
 JEM (6855.T): Mainly specialize in memory probe cards, 5th largest probe card
company by revenue as of year end 2025.
 WinWay (6515.TWO): One of the fastest-growing logic probe card suppliers.
Following its collaboration with Technoprobe, WinWay rose from the 15th-largest
probe card supplier in 2024 to the fifth-largest logic probe card supplier in 2025.
Probe card revenue increased from 11% of sales in 2024 to 29% in 2025.

 Chunghwa Precision Test Tech (6510.TW) : A key supplier for smartphone
application processor (AP) probe cards, and has been migrating to HPC related
applications in recent years. Although its share within the probe card market is
minor, it is a major supplier for probe card PCB, especially for high end probe cards
with advanced PCB that has high layer counts (>40 layers, and over 80 layers for AI
applications). Its key competitive advantage is its vertical integration as it

manufacturers the MEMS probe and PCB in-house.

Probe cards generally employ either vertical or cantilever architectures. Vertical probe
cards use probes arranged perpendicular to the substrate, enabling higher pin density

### Page 10

Asia Semiconductor Insight 8 October 2026 ab 10
and finer pitch capability. They have become the standard for AI, HPC and other
advanced semiconductor applications. Cantilever probe cards use angled probes
extending from the PCB and are typically used in analog, driver IC and selected memory
applications where pin-count and pitch requirements are less demanding. The latest
generation of AI accelerators increasingly relies on MEMS (Micro-Electro-Mechanical

Systems) based vertical probe cards, which support ultra-fine pitches, higher pin counts
and improved probing precision. Technoprobe currently leads this segment with pitch
capability reaching approximately 40 micrometres.
For leading-edge AI applications, probe cards must increasingly support:
1. Higher current-carrying capability (CCC).
2. Stable contact resistance.
3. Lower parasitic inductance for signal integrity.
4. Greater durability under varying thermal conditions.

For CHPT, with their MEMS probe capabilities stemming from their smartphone
application processor probe card supply, they are in a good position to capture the
growing demand for MEMS probe cards as AI/HPC are shifting to MEMS probes. We also
expect probe card content per device to continue rising as AI accelerators grow in die
size, pin count, power consumption and packaging complexity. This should drive secular
growth for both probe card suppliers and upstream component providers, making chip

probing one of the most attractive areas within the semiconductor testing value chain.

Sockets
Semiconductor test sockets are electro-mechanical interfaces that are used post chip
probing and chip packaging process to test the functionality of the chips. Sockets are

placed on to load boards on top of testers during the final test/system level test
processes. Handlers place the chips onto the sockets, and pins inside the sockets enable
the testers to read the signals from the device under test (DUT, i.e. the packaged chips),
with tester signals fanning out  from beneath the PCB load board.
Figure 16: Socket illustration
Source: UBS
Figure 17: FT equipment/
consumables

Tester

Source: UBS

### Page 11

Asia Semiconductor Insight 8 October 2026 ab 11
As AI accelerators become larger and more complex, socket requirements continue to
increase. High-end sockets typically comprise a socket housing, alignment plate,
integrated thermal-control components and contact elements that connect the DUT to
the tester. Spring probes (pogo pins) are the most common choice for HPC sockets, with
high durability, individual probe replacement flexibility, and consistent contact force. FT

and SLT sockets account for more than 70% of the total socket market, with burn-in
sockets representing the remainder.
Compared with the probe card market, the socket industry is more fragmented. Key
suppliers include:
 WinWay (6515.TW) : A major share gainer in AI/HPC sockets, with strong
exposure to Nvidia and AMD. The company specializes in high-performance
coaxial sockets and is developing its next-generation HyperSocket platform. It is
also expanding into probe cards through its partnership with Technoprobe.

 Leeno (058470.KQ) : Vertically integrated supplier focused on smartphone AP
and memory sockets.
 ISC (095340.KQ): Leading memory socket supplier with elastomer expertise.
 Smiths Interconnect (Acquired by Molex) : A key player in AI/HPC socket
industry, with its DaVinci coaxial sockets able to support high frequency
connectivity testing.
 Yamaichi (6941.T): Leading Japanese socket supplier with strong automotive
exposure.
 Yokowo (6800.T): Major provider of contact probes and socket pins.

 Cohu (COHU.O) : Broad test-solutions provider spanning ATE, handlers and
sockets, with greater auto/industrial/consumer  exposure.

AI accelerators are pushing socket requirements beyond 10,000 pins, making coaxial

sockets the industry standard for leading-edge AI/HPC devices. These sockets must
support transmission speeds of up to 224G while handling significantly higher thermal
design power (TDP) during testing.
Elastomer technology is increasingly being adopted alongside spring-probe (pogo-pin)
architectures to reduce chip damage and improve contact reliability. WinWay's next-
generation HyperSocket integrates elastomer materials between the DUT and spring

probes, improving probe lifetime, reducing cleaning requirements and enhancing
production efficiency. We believe HyperSocket could become an important
differentiator as next-generation AI accelerators demand higher pin counts, bandwidth
and thermal performance. We estimate HyperSocket could contribute approximately
5% of WinWay's revenue in 2027E while carrying a 20-30% ASP premium versus
conventional coaxial sockets.
The transition toward larger AI packages, higher pin counts and faster data-transfer

speeds should continue to increase socket content per device. We expect this trend to
support long-term growth in the socket market, with technology leaders such as
WinWay well positioned to gain share through next-generation solutions including

HyperSocket.

Load Board/Interface board
Load boards are specialised printed circuit boards (PCBs) that connect the tester to the
device under test (DUT) during final test (FT) and system-level test (SLT). Acting as the
electrical interface between the packaged chip and automated test equipment (ATE),

load boards are responsible for power delivery and high-speed signal transmission
throughout the testing process. As AI and HPC devices become increasingly complex,
load board requirements continue to rise. Multilayer construction is crucial as modern
load boards often require more than 50 layers for smartphone application processors
and over 80 layers for leading-edge AI accelerators to support higher pin counts, dense
signal routing and greater power delivery demands. At the same time, impedance

control is an important factor as maintaining signal integrity is becoming increasingly
important as interconnect bandwidth rises and high-speed SerDes blocks become more
prevalent. These trends are driving greater technology content and value per load board.
We expect load board content to increase alongside AI chip complexity, larger package
sizes and higher interface speeds, benefiting suppliers with advanced multilayer PCB
capabilities such as CHPT.

Figure 22: CHPT's AP load board
Source: Company data (CHPT)
Figure 23: CHPT's AI load board
Source: Company data (CHPT)
Shift-left igniting secular growth for chip probing
The industry's adoption of shift-left testing should drive a multi-year structural growth
opportunity for chip probing (CP) and its supply chain. As advanced packaging costs rise
and AI accelerators become increasingly complex, semiconductor manufacturers are
placing greater emphasis on identifying known-good dies (KGD) before packaging. This

### Page 13

Asia Semiconductor Insight 8 October 2026 ab 13
underpins our above-consensus forecasts for Chunghwa Precision Test (CHPT), with
2027E/28E sales 7%/15% above consensus driven by structural AI/HPC probe card and
test consumables demand, capacity expansion, and increasing test interface content as
test insertions and test time increase. WinWay should also benefit through its growing
probe card business alongside partner Technoprobe, with probe card sales expected to

grow 94%/72% YoY in 2026E/27E following 161%/260% growth in 2024/25.
Figure 24: CP is set to grow substantially with  shift-left, each packaging step set to be MORE costly than the prior step
RDL / RWAT testing Wafer-level CP (WLCP)
CoWChiplet (if use SoIC) CoW
CP (Die A)
